# Decisions

New rows go at the TOP, for the reader. ⚠️ Position is a CONVENTION, never an invariant, and this
file has never obeyed it consistently — measured 2026-09-14: 255 rows, **71** places where the next
row's id is HIGHER than the one above it. Nothing reads the order (`decisions.py --next-id` takes a
max, `check_decisions_unique.py` takes a set), so a row's position is never a defect and must never be
"fixed" by moving rows — that would edit immutable history to satisfy a preference. What IS enforced:
every `| D-NNN |` row is UNIQUE, sits INSIDE the table (below the `|---|` delimiter — a row above it is
outside the table and the duplicate check's line regex used to bless it), and is IMMUTABLE — a changed
decision gets a NEW row whose what-cell opens "supersedes D-NNN:"; the old row is never edited. WHY ≤ 2 lines; the full rationale
lives at the WHERE links. Subagents and the pipeline never hold the pen — the dispatching session
appends. Query: grep this file first; fleet-wide: `python3 /opt/fabrik/scripts/decisions.py <term>`
(`--check` validates ledger integrity — its own `--help` is the current check inventory).

The union of the slices IS one full pass, every file read once — on this review, ~5 seats per round instead of 10. (4) All four models, each once, where they pay; after round 1 a model sweeps only if ITS slice changed, and most of Haiku's work should become a SCRIPT at close rather than a seat at all. Interface accepted as specced (their `2026-09-02-health-probe-comparison-mode-design.md`, CONVERGED 9 passes, `5f5b2e6f`): `compare(name, expected, actual, *, comparator=None)` returning `{system,status,detail}` + `expected`/`actual`/`match`; `status` stays LIVENESS-only and agreement lives in `match`; `cli(probes, critical=None, *, mismatch_exit=2, strict=False)`. **RATIFIED by us, since we consume the exit code: `2` for mismatch** (0 pass · 1 DOWN · 2 DISAGREES) — a distinct code is what lets the runner tell "prod is dead" from "prod is alive holding the wrong data". **`match` is TRI-STATE and `None` ≠ pass**: it maps to `not checked`, counts in the denominator, never in the numerator. **The runner ALWAYS passes `strict=True`** — `health_probe.py:448` (`critical = critical or set()`) with `:478`/`:481` means an undeclared `critical` makes the CLI `sys.exit(0)` while printing `DOWN:`, so their opt-in flag is load-bearing for the hub. REJECTED, do not re-propose: (a) the old hub-side FALLBACK (vendor as-is + implement the diff in the parity runner) — it would have invented a second comparison shape and then owed a migration; (b) a `MISMATCH` status value — it silently reclassifies a healthy-but-disagreeing provider as unhealthy and `live_chain` DROPS it from the routing chain, an outage caused by a verification feature; (c) a separate fabrik-lib comparison module — the capability needs only the injectable-probe seam and the result shape, both already health-probe's. CLASS: reversible — an interface binding on unbuilt code; both sides can renegotiate before any implementation lands. | our requirement "non-zero exit on mismatch, matching your existing critical-down semantics" was UNREACHABLE as worded, because those semantics already fail OPEN — matching them would have inherited the exact fail-open the requirement existed to prevent; and the boolean `match` we asked for forces a liveness-only probe to report `True`, a false all-clear in precisely the 0-of-760 shape this work exists to catch | docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-deployment-verification-contract-design.md § Amendment 2 · docs/development/plans/archived/2026-09-01-plan-1-deployment-verification.md Phase A steps 5–6 · /opt/fabrik-lib/health-probe/health_probe.py:448,478,481 · mail 01M1G4SWQR · 01M1G798QB · 01M1G8N9DC |

A fourth fact the ask did not raise reshapes the rest: a single observer call flips 13.6% of the time and needs 11 trials for 95% fidelity, i.e. ~80% overhead -- a cheap observer is unreliable and a reliable one unaffordable, so the only sound design judges a minority of runs on few criteria.
